Then, we define the optimistic improvement with w.r.t.
{\bm{x}}^{(n)}
as:

\hat{a}^{(n)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)})=\widehat{\mathrm{UCB}}^{(F)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)})-\max\{\widehat{\mathrm{LCB}}^{(F)}_{t}({\bm{y}}^{(n-1)}),\hat{Q}_{t+n-1}\}.

(D.150)

Furthermore, we define the maximum uncertainty w.r.t.
({\bm{y}}^{(n-1)},{\bm{x}}^{(n)})
as:

\hat{b}^{(n)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)})=\max_{({\bm{x}}^{(n+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)})}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{{\bm{0}},1,t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)}).

(D.151)

From
eqs.

D.150
and
D.151
, the AF
\hat{c}^{(n)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)})
for this setting is given by:

\hat{c}^{(n)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)})=\max\{\hat{a}^{(n)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)}),\eta_{t}\hat{b}^{(n)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)})\},

(D.152)

where
\eta_{t}
is some learning rate tends to zero.
Using this AF
\hat{c}^{(n)}_{t}
, we propose the following selection rule:

\displaystyle{\bm{x}}^{(n)}_{t+n}

\displaystyle=\argmax_{{\bm{x}}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{X}^{(n)}}\hat{c}^{(n)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)}_{t+n-1}),

(D.153)

\displaystyle{\bm{y}}^{(n)}_{t+n}

\displaystyle={\bm{f}}^{(n)}({\bm{y}}^{(n-1)}_{t+n-1},{\bm{x}}^{(n)}_{t+n})+{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(n)}_{t+n},

(D.154)

where
{\bm{y}}^{(0)}_{t}={\bm{0}}
.
Then, the following theorem holds.

Theorem D.14
.

Assume that
D.1
,
C.3
,
C.4
,
D.12
and
D.13
hold.
Also assume that
\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}})\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{(n)}

for any

s\in\{0,\ldots,N-1\}
,
n\in\{s+1,\ldots,N\}
, iteration
t\geq 1
, realization
{\bm{\epsilon}}
, given
{\bm{y}}\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{(s)}
and input
({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})
.
Let
\delta\in(0,1)
and
\xi>0
, and define
\beta_{t}
by
eq.

D.14
and
\eta_{t}=(1+\log t)^{-1}
.
Then, there exists a sequence
0=T_{0}<T_{1}<T_{2}<\cdots
such that
T_{k}\in N\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and

\mathbb{P}(\exists t\in N\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\ \text{s.t.}\ T_{k-1}\leq t\leq T_{k},\ 2C^{2}_{9,t}M_{\text{sum}}\epsilon_{\text{sum},t}<\xi^{2}/2)>1-\frac{6\delta}{\pi^{2}k^{2}}.

(D.155)

Moreover, when the optimization is performed using
eq.

D.154
, the following inequality holds with probability at least
1-2\delta
:

F({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,\ast},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}_{F,\ast})-F(\hat{\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,T_{K}},\ldots,\hat{\bm{x}}^{(N)}_{F,T_{K}})<\xi,

(D.156)

where
T_{K}
is an element of the sequence
\{T_{k}\}_{k=0}^{\infty}
satisfying

\frac{4C^{2}_{9,T_{K}}M^{2}_{\text{sum}}}{\log(1+\sigma^{-2})}\tilde{\gamma}_{T_{K}}K^{-1}<\xi^{2}/2.

(D.157)

In order to prove
theorem

D.14
, we first give four lemmas.

Lemma D.15
.

Assume that the same condition as in
theorem

D.14
holds.
Then, for any
s\in\{1,\ldots,N-1\}
,
n\in\{s+1,\ldots,N\}
,
m\in[M^{(n)}]
, iteration
t\geq 1
, realization
\bm{\epsilon}
and input
{\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}
, the following holds with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle|\sigma^{(n)}_{m,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})-\sigma^{(n)}_{m,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})|

(D.158)

\displaystyle\leq 2M_{\text{prod}}C^{N-1}_{0,t}\sum_{p=0}^{n-s-1}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n-1-p)}}\sigma^{(n-1-p)}_{i,t}\Bigl(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n-2-p)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-2-p)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n-1-p)}\Bigr)

(D.159)

\displaystyle\quad+2M_{\text{prod}}C^{N-1}_{0,t}\sum_{q=1}^{M^{(s)}}|\epsilon^{(s)}_{q}|.

(D.160)

Proof.

By using the same argument as in the proof of
lemma

C.11
, the following holds with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle|\sigma^{(n)}_{m,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})-\sigma^{(n)}_{m,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})|

(D.161)

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta^{1/2}_{t}L_{\sigma}\sum_{j=1}^{M^{(n-1)}}\sigma^{(n-1)}_{j,t}\Bigl(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n-2)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-2)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}\Bigr)+L_{\sigma}M^{(n-1)}(L_{f}+\beta^{1/2}_{t}L_{\sigma})

(D.162)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n-2)}}\Bigl[|\tilde{z}^{(n-2)}_{{\bm{0}},i,t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-2)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s-1)})-\tilde{z}^{(n-2)}_{{\bm{0}},i,t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-2)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})|\Bigr]

(D.163)

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta^{1/2}_{t}L_{\sigma}\sum_{j=1}^{M^{(n-1)}}\sigma^{(n-1)}_{j,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n-2)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-2)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)})

(D.164)

\displaystyle\quad+2\beta^{1/2}_{t}L_{\sigma}M^{(n-1)}(L_{f}+\beta^{1/2}_{t}L_{\sigma})\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n-2)}}\sigma^{(n-2)}_{i,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n-3)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-3)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n-2)})

(D.165)

\displaystyle\quad+L_{\sigma}M^{(n-1)}M^{(n-2)}(L_{f}+\beta^{1/2}_{t}L_{\sigma})^{2}

(D.166)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sum_{q=1}^{M^{(n-3)}}\Bigl[|\tilde{z}^{(n-3)}_{{\bm{0}},q,t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-3)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s-1)})-\tilde{z}^{(n-3)}_{{\bm{0}},q,t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-3)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s)})|\Bigr]

(D.167)

\displaystyle\leq

(D.168)

(D.169)

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta^{1/2}_{t}L_{\sigma}M_{\text{prod}}(L_{f}+\beta^{1/2}_{t}L_{\sigma}+1)^{N-2}

(D.170)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sum_{p=0}^{n-s-2}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n-1-p)}}\Bigl[\sigma^{(n-1-p)}_{i,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n-2-p)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-2-p)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(n-1-p)})\Bigr]

(D.171)

\displaystyle\quad+2M_{\text{prod}}L_{\sigma}(L_{f}+\beta^{1/2}_{t}L_{\sigma}+1)^{N-2}\sum_{q=1}^{M^{(s)}}\Bigl[|\mu^{(s)}_{q,t}({\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}},{\bm{x}}^{(s)})-f^{(s)}_{q}({\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}},{\bm{x}}^{(s)})-\epsilon^{(s)}_{q}|\Bigr]

(D.172)

\displaystyle\leq 2M_{\text{prod}}(L_{f}+\beta^{1/2}_{t}L_{\sigma}+1)^{N-1}

(D.173)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sum_{p=0}^{n-s-1}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n-1-p)}}\Bigl[\sigma^{(n-1-p)}_{i,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n-2-p)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-2-p)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(n-1-p)})\Bigr]

(D.174)

\displaystyle\quad+2M_{\text{prod}}(L_{f}+\beta^{1/2}_{t}L_{\sigma}+1)^{N-1}\sum_{q=1}^{M^{(s)}}|\epsilon^{(s)}_{q}|

(D.175)

\displaystyle=2M_{\text{prod}}C_{0,t}^{N-1}\sum_{p=0}^{n-s-1}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n-1-p)}}\Bigl[\sigma^{(n-1-p)}_{i,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(n-2-p)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-2-p)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(n-1-p)})\Bigr]

(D.176)

\displaystyle\quad+2M_{\text{prod}}C_{0,t}^{N-1}\sum_{q=1}^{M^{(s)}}|\epsilon^{(s)}_{q}|.

(D.177)

∎

Lemma D.16
.

Assume that the same condition as in
theorem

D.14
holds.
Then, for any
s\in\{1,\ldots,N-1\}
,
j\geq 0
with
s+j\leq N
, iteration
t\geq 1
, realization
\bm{\epsilon}
and input
{\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}
, the following holds with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-j)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-j)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})

(D.178)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2,t}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-j)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-j)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})+C_{2,t}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-j-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-j-1)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})

(D.179)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{2,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}\sigma^{(s)}_{i,t}({\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}},{\bm{x}}^{(s)})+C_{2,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}|\epsilon^{(s)}_{i}|,

(D.180)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-j)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-j)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})

(D.181)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=j}^{N-s}\prod_{q=1}^{p}M^{(N-q+1)}L_{f}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(N-p)}}\Bigl[\sigma^{(N-p)}_{i,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(N-p-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-p-1)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(N-p)})\Bigr].

(D.182)

Proof.

From the definition of
\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-j)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-j)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})
,
the following inequality holds with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-j)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-j)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})

(D.183)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=j}^{N-s}\prod_{q=1}^{p}M^{(N-q+1)}L_{f}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(N-p)}}\Bigl[\sigma^{(N-p)}_{i,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(N-p-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-p-1)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(N-p)})\Bigr]

(D.184)

\displaystyle\leq M_{\text{prod}}C^{N-1}_{0}\sum_{p=j}^{N-s}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(N-p)}}\Bigl[\sigma^{(N-p)}_{i,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(N-p-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-p-1)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(N-p)})\Bigr]

(D.185)

\displaystyle=M_{\text{prod}}C^{N-1}_{0}\sum_{p=j}^{N-s-1}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(N-p)}}\Bigl[\sigma^{(N-p)}_{i,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(N-p-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-p-1)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(N-p)})\Bigr]

(D.186)

\displaystyle\quad+M_{\text{prod}}C^{N-1}_{0}\sum_{p=j}^{N-s-1}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(N-p)}}\Bigl[\left(\sigma^{(N-p)}_{i,t}\Bigl(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(N-p-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-p-1)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(N-p)}\Bigr)\Bigr]\right.

(D.187)

\displaystyle\quad\left.-\sigma^{(N-p)}_{i,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(N-p-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-p-1)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}),{\bm{x}}^{(N-p)})\right)+M_{\text{prod}}C^{N-1}_{0}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}\sigma^{(s)}_{i,t}({\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}},{\bm{x}}^{(s)}).

(D.188)

Hence, from
lemma

D.15
, it follows that

\displaystyle|\sigma^{(N-p)}_{i,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(N-p-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s:N-p-1)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(N-p)})-\sigma^{(N-p)}_{i,t}(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(N-p-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1:N-p-1)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s)}),{\bm{x}}^{(N-p)})|

(D.189)

\displaystyle\leq 2M_{\text{prod}}C^{N-1}_{0}\sum_{r=0}^{N-p-s-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M^{(N-p-1-r)}}\Bigl[\sigma^{(N-p-1-r)}_{j,t}\Bigl(\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(N-p-2-r)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s:N-p-2-r)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(N-p-1-r)}\Bigr)\Bigr]

(D.190)

\displaystyle\quad+2M_{\text{prod}}C^{N-1}_{0}\sum_{q=1}^{M^{(s)}}|\epsilon^{(s)}_{q}|.

(D.191)

Hence, using the same argument as in the proof of
lemma

C.12
, we have the desired result.
∎

Lemma D.17
.

Assume that the same condition as in
theorem

D.14
holds.
Then, for any
s\in\{1,\ldots,N-1\}
, iteration
t\geq 1
, realization
\bm{\epsilon}
and input
{\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}
, the following holds with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{{\bm{0}},1,t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}|{\bm{z}}_{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(s-1)})

(D.192)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{3,t}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{{\bm{0}},1,t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})+C_{3,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}\sigma^{(s)}_{i,t}({\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}},{\bm{x}}^{(s)})+C_{3,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}|\epsilon^{(s)}_{i}|.

(D.193)

Proof.

By repeating
lemma

D.16
, the following holds with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{{\bm{0}},1,t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})

(D.194)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-0)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-0)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})

(D.195)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2,t}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-0)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-0)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})+C_{2,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}\sigma^{(s)}_{i,t}({\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}},{\bm{x}}^{(s)})+C_{2,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}|\epsilon^{(s)}_{i}|

(D.196)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{2,t}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-1)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})

(D.197)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2,t}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-0)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-0)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})+C_{2,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}\sigma^{(s)}_{i,t}({\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}},{\bm{x}}^{(s)})+C_{2,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}|\epsilon^{(s)}_{i}|

(D.198)

\displaystyle\quad+C^{2}_{2,t}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-1)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-1)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})+C^{2}_{2,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}\sigma^{(s)}_{i,t}({\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}},{\bm{x}}^{(s)})+C^{2}_{2,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}|\epsilon^{(s)}_{i}|

(D.199)

\displaystyle\quad+C^{2}_{2,t}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-2)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-2)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})

(D.200)

\displaystyle\leq

(D.201)

(D.202)

\displaystyle\leq(C_{2,t}+C^{2}_{2,t}+\cdots+C^{N}_{2,t})\tilde{\sigma}^{(N-0)}_{{\bm{0}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N-0)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})

(D.203)

\displaystyle\quad+(C_{2,t}+C^{2}_{2,t}+\cdots+C^{N}_{2,t})\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}\sigma^{(s)}_{i,t}({\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}},{\bm{x}}^{(s)})+(C_{2,t}+C^{2}_{2,t}+\cdots+C^{N}_{2,t})\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}|\epsilon^{(s)}_{i}|

(D.204)

\displaystyle\leq NC^{N}_{2,t}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{{\bm{0}},1,t}({\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}|{\bm{z}}^{(s)}_{\bm{\epsilon}})+NC^{N}_{2,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}\sigma^{(s)}_{i,t}({\bm{z}}^{(s-1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}},{\bm{x}}^{(s)})+NC^{N}_{2,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(s)}}|\epsilon^{(s)}_{i}|.

(D.205)

∎

Lemma D.18
.

Assume that the same condition as in
theorem

D.14
holds.
Then, for any
n\in[N]
, iteration
t\geq 1
,
{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)}\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{(n-1)}
and input
{\bm{x}}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{X}^{(n)}
, the following holds:

\eta_{t}\hat{b}^{(n)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)})\leq\hat{c}^{(n)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)})\leq(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+\eta_{t})\hat{b}^{(n)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(n)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)}).

(D.206)

Proof.

By using the same argument as in the proof of
lemma

C.14
, we get
lemma

D.18
.
∎

Using these lemmas we prove
theorem

D.14
.

Proof.

Let
t\in N\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
Then, from
lemma

D.18
,
{\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{t+1}
satisfies that

\hat{c}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{t+1}|{\bm{0}})\leq(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+\eta_{t})\hat{b}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{t+1}|{\bm{0}})=(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+\eta_{t})\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{{\bm{0}},1,t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{t+1},\tilde{\bm{x}}^{(2)},\ldots,\tilde{\bm{x}}^{(N)}|{\bm{0}}).

(D.207)

In addition, from
eq.

D.207
and
lemma

D.17
, using the same argument as in the proof of
theorem

C.10
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,
\hat{c}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{t+1}|{\bm{0}})
can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{c}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{t+1}|{\bm{0}})

\displaystyle\leq(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+\eta_{t})C_{3,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(1)}}\sigma^{(1)}_{{\bm{0}},i,t}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{t+1})+(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+\eta_{t})C_{3,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(1)}}|\epsilon^{(1)}_{i}|

(D.208)

\displaystyle\qquad+(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+\eta_{t})C_{3,t}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{{\bm{0}},1,t}(\tilde{\bm{x}}^{(2)},\ldots,\tilde{\bm{x}}^{(N)}|{\bm{y}}^{(1)}_{t+1})

(D.209)

\displaystyle\leq(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+\eta_{t})C_{3,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(1)}}\sigma^{(1)}_{{\bm{0}},i,t}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{t+1})+(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+\eta_{t})C_{3,t}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(1)}}|\epsilon^{(1)}_{i}|

(D.210)

\displaystyle\qquad+(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+\eta_{t})^{2}C_{3,t}\eta^{-1}_{t}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{{\bm{0}},1,t}({\bm{x}}^{(2)}_{t+2},\ldots,\tilde{\bm{x}}^{(N)}|{\bm{y}}^{(1)}_{t+1}).

(D.211)

By using
lemma

D.17
again, it follows that

\displaystyle\hat{c}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{t+1}|{\bm{0}})

\displaystyle\leq(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+\eta_{t}+1)^{N}C^{N}_{3,t}\eta^{-N}_{t}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n)}}\sigma^{(n)}_{{\bm{0}},i,t}({\bm{y}}^{(n-1)},{\bm{x}}^{(n)}_{t+n})

(D.212)

\displaystyle\qquad+(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+\eta_{t}+1)^{N}C^{N}_{3,t}\eta^{-N}_{t}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n)}}|\epsilon^{(n)}_{i}|

(D.213)

\displaystyle\leq(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+1+1)^{N}C^{N}_{3,t}\eta^{-N}_{t}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n)}}\sigma^{(n)}_{{\bm{0}},i,t}({\bm{y}}^{(n-1)},{\bm{x}}^{(n)}_{t+n})

(D.214)

\displaystyle\qquad+(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+1+1)^{N}C^{N}_{3,t}\eta^{-N}_{t}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n)}}|\epsilon^{(n)}_{i}|.

(D.215)

Thus, multiplying both sides by
2\beta^{1/2}_{t}\eta^{-1}_{t}
, we get

\displaystyle 2\beta^{1/2}_{t}\eta^{-1}_{t}\hat{c}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{t+1}|{\bm{0}})

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta^{1/2}_{t}\eta^{-1}_{t}(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+2)^{N}C^{N}_{3,t}\eta^{-N}_{t}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n)}}\sigma^{(n)}_{{\bm{0}},i,t}({\bm{y}}^{(n-1)},{\bm{x}}^{(n)}_{t+n})

(D.216)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\beta^{1/2}_{t}\eta^{-1}_{t}(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}+2)^{N}C^{N}_{3,t}\eta^{-N}_{t}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n)}}|\epsilon^{(n)}_{i}|

(D.217)

\displaystyle=C_{9,t}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n)}}\sigma^{(n)}_{{\bm{0}},i,t}({\bm{y}}^{(n-1)},{\bm{x}}^{(n)}_{t+n})+C_{9,t}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n)}}|\epsilon^{(n)}_{i}|.

(D.218)

Here, using
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we obtain

(2\beta^{1/2}_{t}\eta^{-1}_{t}\hat{c}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{t+1}|{\bm{0}}))^{2}\leq 2C^{2}_{9,t}M_{\text{sum}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n)}}\sigma^{(n)2}_{{\bm{0}},i,t}({\bm{y}}^{(n-1)},{\bm{x}}^{(n)}_{t+n})+2C^{2}_{9,t}M_{\text{sum}}\epsilon_{\text{sum}}.

(D.219)

Next, we show the existence of the sequence
T_{0}<T_{1}<\cdots
satisfying

\mathbb{P}(\exists t\in N\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\ \text{s.t.}\ T_{k-1}\leq t\leq T_{k},\ 2C^{2}_{9,t}M_{\text{sum}}\epsilon_{\text{sum},t}<\xi^{2}/2)>1-\frac{6\delta}{\pi^{2}k^{2}}.

(D.220)

From
D.12
, we have

\mathbb{P}(2C^{2}_{9,t}M_{\text{sum}}\epsilon_{\text{sum},t}<\xi^{2}/2)=\mathbb{P}(\epsilon_{\text{sum},t}<M^{-1}_{\text{sum}}C^{-2}_{9,t}\xi^{2}/4)>\frac{C\xi^{2}}{4M_{\text{sum}}C^{2}_{9,t}}.

(D.221)

This implies that

1-\mathbb{P}(2C^{2}_{9,t}M_{\text{sum}}\epsilon_{\text{sum},t}<\xi^{2}/2)\leq 1-\frac{C\xi^{2}}{4M_{\text{sum}}C^{2}_{9,t}}.

(D.222)

Therefore, using
1+x\leq e^{x}
we get

\displaystyle\prod_{q=t}^{t^{\prime}}(1-\mathbb{P}(2C^{2}_{9,q}M_{\text{sum}}\epsilon_{\text{sum},q}<\xi^{2}/2))

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{q=t}^{t^{\prime}}\left(1-\frac{C\xi^{2}}{4M_{\text{sum}}C^{2}_{9,q}}\right)

(D.223)

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{q=t}^{t^{\prime}}\exp\left(-\frac{C\xi^{2}}{4M_{\text{sum}}C^{2}_{9,q}}\right)

(D.224)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{C\xi^{2}}{4M_{\text{sum}}}\sum_{q=t}^{t^{\prime}}C^{-2}_{9,q}\right).

(D.225)

Moreover, from
D.13
,
the right hand side of
eq.

D.225
tends to zero when

t^{\prime}\to\infty
.
Thus, we can construct the sequence
T_{1},T_{2},\ldots
satisfying
eq.

D.155
.
Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the following holds:

\forall k\in\mathbb{N},\ \exists\tilde{T}_{k}\quad\mathrm{s.t.}\quad T_{k-1}\leq\tilde{T}_{k}\leq T_{k},\ 2C^{2}_{9,\tilde{T}_{k}}M_{\text{sum}}\epsilon_{\text{sum},\tilde{T}_{k}}<\xi^{2}/2.

(D.226)

On the other hand, for the positive number
K
satisfying the theorem’s inequality, we define

\hat{T}=\argmin_{1\leq k\leq K}(2\beta^{1/2}_{\tilde{T}_{k}}\eta^{-1}_{\tilde{T}_{k}}\hat{c}^{(1)}_{\tilde{T}_{k}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{{\tilde{T}_{k}}+1}|{\bm{0}}))^{2}.

(D.227)

Then, it follows that

\displaystyle K(2\beta^{1/2}_{\hat{T}}\eta^{-1}_{\hat{T}}\hat{c}^{(1)}_{\hat{T}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{{\hat{T}}+1}|{\bm{0}}))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}(2\beta^{1/2}_{\tilde{T}_{k}}\eta^{-1}_{\tilde{T}_{k}}\hat{c}^{(1)}_{\tilde{T}_{k}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{{\tilde{T}_{k}}+1}|{\bm{0}}))^{2}

(D.228)

\displaystyle\leq K\xi^{2}/2+2C^{2}_{9,T_{K}}M_{\text{sum}}\sum_{t=1}^{T_{K}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{M^{(n)}}\sigma^{(n)2}_{{\bm{0}},i,t}({\bm{y}}^{(n-1)},{\bm{x}}^{(n)}_{t+n})

(D.229)

\displaystyle\leq K\xi^{2}/2+\frac{4C^{2}_{9,T_{K}}M^{2}_{\text{sum}}}{\log(1+\sigma^{-2})}\tilde{\gamma}_{T_{K}}.

(D.230)

By dividing both sides by
K
, we obtain

\displaystyle(2\beta^{1/2}_{\hat{T}}\eta^{-1}_{\hat{T}}\hat{c}^{(1)}_{\hat{T}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{{\hat{T}}+1}|{\bm{0}}))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\xi^{2}/2+\frac{4C^{2}_{9,T_{K}}M^{2}_{\text{sum}}}{\log(1+\sigma^{-2})}\tilde{\gamma}_{T_{K}}K^{-1}

(D.231)

\displaystyle<\xi^{2}/2+\xi^{2}/2=\xi^{2}.

(D.232)

This implies that

2\beta^{1/2}_{\hat{T}}\eta^{-1}_{\hat{T}}\hat{c}^{(1)}_{\hat{T}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{{\hat{T}}+1}|{\bm{0}})<\xi.

(D.233)

Finally, from the definition of the estimated solution and CIs,
F(\cdot)
can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle F({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,\ast},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}_{F,\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\min_{t\in N\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},t\leq T_{K}}\widehat{\mathrm{UCB}}^{(F)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,\ast},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}_{F,\ast})

(D.234)

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathrm{UCB}}^{(F)}_{\hat{T}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,\ast},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}_{F,\ast}),

(D.235)

\displaystyle F(\hat{\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,T_{K}},\ldots,\hat{\bm{x}}^{(N)}_{F,T_{K}})

\displaystyle\geq\max_{t\in N\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},t\leq T_{K}}\widehat{\mathrm{LCB}}^{(F)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)})

(D.236)

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{\mathrm{LCB}}^{(F)}_{\hat{T}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,\ast},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}_{F,\ast}).

(D.237)

Therefore, the following holds with probability at least
1-2\delta
:

\displaystyle F({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,\ast},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}_{F,\ast})-F(\hat{\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,T_{K}},\ldots,\hat{\bm{x}}^{(N)}_{F,T_{K}})

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta^{1/2}_{\hat{T}}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{{\bm{0}},1,\hat{T}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,\ast},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(N)}_{F,\ast})

(D.238)

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta^{1/2}_{\hat{T}}\hat{b}^{(1)}_{\hat{T}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,\ast}|{\bm{0}})

(D.239)

\displaystyle\leq 2{\beta}^{1/2}_{\hat{T}}\eta^{-1}_{\hat{T}}\hat{c}^{(1)}_{\hat{T}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{F,\ast}|{\bm{0}})\leq 2{\beta}^{1/2}_{\hat{T}}\eta^{-1}_{\hat{T}}\hat{c}^{(1)}_{\hat{T}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}_{\hat{T}+1}|{\bm{0}}).

(D.240)

Hence, by substituting
eq.

D.233
into
eq.

D.240
, we have
theorem

D.14
.
∎

E
Sufficient Conditions and Modifications for the Proposed Method

In this section, we consider theorem’s conditions and its modifications.
First, in the noiseless setting, we assume that
\tilde{\bm{\mu}}_{t}^{(m)}({\bm{x}}^{(n)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(m)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)})\in\mathcal{Y}^{(m)}
to construct the valid CI
.
For this assumption, the following sufficient condition exists.

Theorem E.1
.

Assume that each
\mathcal{X}^{(n)}
is a compact set, and each observation is noiseless.
Also assume that each
f^{(n)}_{m}
is a function defined on
[-2B_{n-1},2B_{n-1}]^{M^{(n-1)}}\times\mathcal{X}^{(n)}
and satisfies
f^{(n)}_{m}\in\mathcal{H}_{k^{(n)}}
, where
B_{n}
is some positive constant satisfying
\|f^{(n)}_{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k^{(n)}}}\leq B_{n}
and
B_{0}=0
.
Then,
\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{(n)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})\in[-2B_{n},2B_{n}]^{M^{(n)}}

for any
n\in[N]
,
t\geq 1
and
{\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)}
.

Proof.

From the reproducing property of
k^{(n)}
, noting that
k^{(n)}({\bm{a}},{\bm{a}})\leq 1
we have

\displaystyle|f^{(1)}_{m}({\bm{a}})|

\displaystyle=|\langle f^{(1)}_{m}(\cdot),k^{(1)}(\cdot,{\bm{a}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{k^{(1)}}}|

(E.1)

\displaystyle\leq\|f^{(1)}_{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k^{(1)}}}k^{(1)}({\bm{a}},{\bm{a}})^{1/2}\leq B_{1}.

(E.2)

In addition, since
\mathcal{X}^{(1)}
is the compact set,
[-2B_{0},2B_{0}]^{M^{(0)}}\times\mathcal{X}^{(1)}
is also the compact set.
Hence, from
eq.

C.1
the following holds for any
m\in[M^{(1)}]
,
t\geq 1
and
({\bm{w}},{\bm{x}})\in[-2B_{0},2B_{0}]^{M^{(0)}}\times\mathcal{X}^{(1)}
:

\displaystyle|\mu^{(1)}_{m,t}({\bm{w}},{\bm{x}})|

\displaystyle=|\mu^{(1)}_{m,t}({\bm{w}},{\bm{x}})-f^{(n)}_{m}({\bm{w}},{\bm{x}})+f^{(n)}_{m}({\bm{w}},{\bm{x}})|

(E.3)

\displaystyle\leq|\mu^{(1)}_{m,t}({\bm{w}},{\bm{x}})-f^{(n)}_{m}({\bm{w}},{\bm{x}})|+|f^{(n)}_{m}({\bm{w}},{\bm{x}})|

(E.4)

\displaystyle\leq B_{1}+B_{1}=2B_{1}.

(E.5)

This implies that
{\bm{\mu}}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)})=\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)})\in[-2B_{1},2B_{1}]^{M^{(1)}}
.
By repeating this process, we get

\displaystyle{\bm{\mu}}^{(n)}_{t}(\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{(n-1)}_{t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})=\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{(n)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})\in[-2B_{n},2B_{n}]^{M^{(n)}}.

(E.6)

∎

Theorem

E.1
implies that by defining
\mathcal{Y}^{(n)}
as
[-2B_{n},2B_{n}]^{M^{(n)}}
and

B=\max_{1\leq n\leq N}B_{n}
, we obtain
C.1
and
\tilde{\bm{\mu}}_{t}^{(n)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})\in\mathcal{Y}^{(n)}
. Similarly, under the same assumption we have
\tilde{\bm{\mu}}_{t}^{(m)}({\bm{x}}^{(n)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(m)}|{\bm{y}}^{(n-1)})\in\mathcal{Y}^{(m)}
.

Next, we consider the condition
\tilde{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(n)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{(n)}

for the noisy observation setting.
In the noisy setting, it is not easy to give a sufficient condition for this condition to be satisfied.
Nevertheless, we can avoid this condition by modifying the definition of
\tilde{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(n)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})
.
Let
\mathcal{L}=[-L,L]^{d}
be a
d
-dimensional hypercube.
For each
{\bm{a}}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, suppose that
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M},{\bm{a}})
is a
projection of
{\bm{a}}
onto
\mathcal{M}
, where the
i
-th element of
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M},{\bm{a}})
,
\mathcal{P}_{i}(\mathcal{M},{\bm{a}})
, is given by

\mathcal{P}_{i}(\mathcal{M},{\bm{a}})=\argmin_{l\in[-L,L]}|a_{i}-l|.

(E.7)

Then, the following theorem holds.

Theorem E.2
.

Assume that each
\mathcal{X}^{(n)}
is a compact set, and each observation noise
\epsilon^{(n)}_{m}
is a zero mean random variable with
-A\leq\epsilon^{(n)}_{m}\leq A
.
Also assume that each
f^{(n)}_{m}
is a function defined on
[-A_{n-1}-B_{n-1},A_{n-1}+B_{n-1}]^{M^{(n-1)}}\times\mathcal{X}^{(n)}
and satisfies
f^{(n)}_{m}\in\mathcal{H}_{k^{(n)}}
, where
A_{0}=B_{0}=0
,
A_{n}=A
and
B_{n}
is some positive constant satisfying
\|f^{(n)}_{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k^{(n)}}}\leq B_{n}
.
For each
t\geq 1
and
({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})
, define

\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})=\begin{cases}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(1)}+\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Y}^{(1)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)}))&(n=1),\\
{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(n)}+\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Y}^{(n)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(n)}_{t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1:n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)}))&(n\geq 2),\end{cases}

(E.8)

where
\mathcal{Y}^{(n)}=[-B_{n},B_{n}]^{M^{(n)}}
.
Then,
\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})\in[-A_{n}-B_{n},A_{n}+B_{n}]^{M^{(n)}}=\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{(n)}
for all
n\in[N]
,
t\geq 1
,
{\bm{\epsilon}}
and
({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})
.
Moreover,
{\bm{f}}^{(n)}
satisfies
{\bm{f}}^{(n)}({\bm{w}},{\bm{x}})+{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(n)}\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{(n)}

for all
{\bm{w}}\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{(n-1)}
,
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{(n)}
and
{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(n)}
.

Proof.

From the reproducing property of
k^{(n)}(\cdot,\cdot)
, and the assumptions
k^{(n)}(\cdot,\cdot)\leq 1
and
\|f^{(n)}_{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k^{(n)}}}\leq B_{n}
,
we have
f^{(n)}_{m}({\bm{w}},{\bm{x}})\in[-B_{n},B_{n}]
.
Therefore, noting that
-A\leq\epsilon^{(n)}_{m}\leq A
, we get
{\bm{f}}^{(n)}
satisfies
{\bm{f}}^{(n)}({\bm{w}},{\bm{x}})+{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(n)}\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{(n)}
.
Similarly, from the definition of
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Y}^{(n)},{\bm{a}})
, it follows that

\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Y}^{(n)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(n)}_{t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)}))\in\mathcal{Y}^{(n)}=[-B_{n},B_{n}]^{M^{(n)}}.

(E.9)

Thus, we obtain
\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}^{(n)}}
.
∎

In this modified
\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})
,
similar results given in
theorem

D.4
hold.

Theorem E.3
.

Assume that the same condition as in
theorem

E.2
holds.
Given
\delta\in(0,1)
, define
B=\max_{1\leq n\leq N}B_{n}
and
\beta_{t}
as in
eq.

D.14
.
Moreover, assume that
C.3
and
C.4
hold.
Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the following holds for any realization of
{\bm{\epsilon}}
:

\displaystyle|{z}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},m}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})-\hat{z}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},m,t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})|\leq

\displaystyle{\beta}^{1/2}_{t}\hat{\sigma}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},m,t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})

(E.10)

\displaystyle\quad\forall n\in[N],m\in[M^{(n)}],t\geq 1,

(E.11)

where
\hat{z}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},m,t}
is the
m
-th element of

\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}
, and
\hat{\sigma}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},m,t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})
is given by

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}^{(n)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},m,t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})=

(E.12)

\displaystyle\quad{\sigma}^{(n)}_{m,t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})+L_{f}\sum_{s=1}^{M^{(n-1)}}\hat{\sigma}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},s,t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),

(E.13)

and
\hat{\sigma}^{(1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},s,t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)})=\sigma^{(1)}_{s,t}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)})
.

Proof.

For any
t\geq 1
,
n\in[N]
,
m\in[M^{(n)}]
,
{\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)}
and realization of
{\bm{\epsilon}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle|f^{(n)}_{m}(\hat{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(n-1)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})-{\mu}^{(n)}_{m,t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})|

(E.14)

\displaystyle=|f^{(n)}_{m}(\hat{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(n-1)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})-\mathcal{P}_{m}(\mathcal{Y}^{(n)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(n)}_{t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)}))|

(E.15)

\displaystyle\quad+|\mathcal{P}_{m}(\mathcal{Y}^{(n)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(n)}_{t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)}))-{\mu}^{(n)}_{m,t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})|

(E.16)

\displaystyle\geq|f^{(n)}_{m}(\hat{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(n-1)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})-\mathcal{P}_{m}(\mathcal{Y}^{(n)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(n)}_{t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(n-1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)}))|,

(E.17)

where the first equality is derived by
f^{(n)}_{m}(\hat{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(n-1)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n-1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(n)})\in\mathcal{Y}^{(n)}
and the definition of
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Y}^{(n)},{\bm{a}})
.
Thus, for

i\in[M^{(2)}]
and
({\bm{x}}^{(1)},{\bm{x}}^{(2)})
, the following inequality holds with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle|z^{(2)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},i}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},{\bm{x}}^{(2)})-\hat{z}^{(2)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},i,t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},{\bm{x}}^{(2)})|

(E.18)

\displaystyle\leq|f^{(2)}_{i}({\bm{z}}^{(1)}_{\bm{\epsilon}}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(2)})-f^{(2)}_{i}(\hat{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(1)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(2)})|

(E.19)

\displaystyle\qquad+|f^{(2)}_{i}(\hat{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(1)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(2)})-\mathcal{P}_{i}(\mathcal{Y}^{(2)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(2)}_{t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(2)}))|

(E.20)

\displaystyle\leq L_{f}\|{\bm{f}}^{(1)}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)})-\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Y}^{(1)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)}))\|_{1}

(E.21)

\displaystyle\qquad+|f^{(2)}_{i}(\hat{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(1)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(2)})-\mathcal{P}_{i}(\mathcal{Y}^{(2)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(2)}_{t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(2)}))|

(E.22)

\displaystyle\leq L_{f}\sum_{j=1}^{M^{(1)}}|{f}_{j}^{(1)}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)})-\mathcal{P}_{j}(\mathcal{Y}^{(1)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(1)}_{t}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)}))|

(E.23)

\displaystyle\qquad+|f^{(2)}_{i}(\hat{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(1)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(2)})-\mathcal{P}_{i}(\mathcal{Y}^{(2)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(2)}_{t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(2)}))|

(E.24)

\displaystyle\leq L_{f}\sum_{j=1}^{M^{(1)}}|{f}_{j}^{(1)}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)})-{\mu}^{(1)}_{j,t}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)})|

(E.25)

\displaystyle\qquad+|f^{(2)}_{i}(\hat{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(1)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(2)})-{\mu}^{(2)}_{i,t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(2)})|

(E.26)

\displaystyle\leq\beta^{1/2}_{t}\sigma^{(2)}_{i,t}(\hat{\bm{z}}^{(1)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)}),{\bm{x}}^{(2)})+L_{f}\beta^{1/2}_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M^{(1)}}\sigma^{(1)}_{j,t}({\bm{0}},{\bm{x}}^{(1)})

(E.27)

\displaystyle=\beta^{1/2}_{t}\hat{\sigma}^{(2)}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},i,t}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},{\bm{x}}^{(2)}).

(E.28)

Therefore, by repeating this process up to
n
, we get the desired inequality.
∎

We emphasize that by using the same technique as used in this proof, it can also be shown that
theorems

D.6
,
D.9
and
D.14
hold when using
\hat{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(n)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})
instead of
\tilde{\bm{z}}_{{\bm{\epsilon}},t}^{(n)}({\bm{x}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{x}}^{(n)})
.

Finally, we provide the sufficient condition for the Lipschitz continuity assumption (L2).

Theorem E.4
.

Let
k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}):\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be one of the following kernel functions:

Linear kernel:

k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})=a^{2}{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{y}}
, where
a
is a positive parameter.

Gaussian kernel:

k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})=a^{2}\exp(-\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|^{2}/(2\rho^{2}))
, where
a
and
\rho
are positive parameters.

Matérn kernel:

k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})=a^{2}\frac{2^{1-\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)}\left(\sqrt{2\nu}\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|}{\rho}\right)^{\nu}K_{\nu}\left(\sqrt{2\nu}\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|}{\rho}\right),

where
a
and
\rho
are positive parameters,
\nu
is a degree of freedom with
\nu>1
,
\Gamma
is the gamma function, and
K_{\nu}
is the modified Bessel function of the second kind.

Moreover, assume that a user-specified variance parameter
\sigma^{2}
is positive.
Then, for any
t\geq 1
and observed points
{\bm{x}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{x}}_{t}
, the posterior standard deviation
\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})
satisfies that

{}^{\forall}{\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ |\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})|\leq C\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{1},

(E.29)

where
C
is a positive constant given by

\displaystyle C=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}a&\text{if}\ k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\ \text{is {\ the \ linear kernel}},\\
\frac{\sqrt{2}a}{\rho}&\text{if}\ k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\ \text{is {\ the \ Gaussian kernel}},\\
\frac{\sqrt{2}a}{\rho}\sqrt{\frac{\nu}{\nu-1}}&\text{if}\ k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\ \text{is {\ the \ Mat\'{e}rn kernel}}.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

First, we show the case of the linear kernel.
Let the matrix
{\bm{X}}_{t}
be
{\bm{X}}_{t}=({\bm{x}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{x}}_{t})^{\top}
.
Then,
\sigma^{2}_{t}({\bm{x}})
is given by

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{t}({\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=a^{2}{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{x}}-a^{4}{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}^{\top}_{t}(a^{2}{\bm{X}}_{t}{\bm{X}}^{\top}_{t}+\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{t})^{-1}{\bm{X}}_{t}{\bm{x}}

\displaystyle=a^{2}{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{x}}-a^{2}{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}^{\top}_{t}({\bm{X}}_{t}{\bm{X}}^{\top}_{t}+a^{-2}\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{t})^{-1}{\bm{X}}_{t}{\bm{x}}

(E.33)

\displaystyle=a^{2}{\bm{x}}^{\top}({\bm{I}}_{d}-{\bm{X}}^{\top}_{t}({\bm{X}}_{t}{\bm{X}}^{\top}_{t}+a^{-2}\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{t})^{-1}{\bm{X}}_{t}){\bm{x}}.

The matrix
{\bm{X}}_{t}
can be decomposed as

{\bm{X}}_{t}={\bm{H}}^{\prime}{\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{H}}^{\top},

where
{\bm{H}}^{\prime}=({\bm{h}}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{h}}^{\prime}_{t})^{\top}
and

{\bm{H}}=({\bm{h}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{h}}_{d})^{\top}
are orthogonal matrices, and
{\bm{\Lambda}}
is the
t\times d
rectangular diagonal matrix whose
(j,j)
element is the
j
th singular value
s_{j}\geq 0
of
{\bm{X}}_{t}
.
Thus,

{\bm{I}}_{d}-{\bm{X}}^{\top}_{t}({\bm{X}}_{t}{\bm{X}}^{\top}_{t}+a^{-2}\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{t})^{-1}{\bm{X}}_{t}
can be rewritten as follows:

{\bm{I}}_{d}-{\bm{X}}^{\top}_{t}({\bm{X}}_{t}{\bm{X}}^{\top}_{t}+a^{-2}\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{t})^{-1}{\bm{X}}_{t}={\bm{H}}{\bm{\Theta}}{\bm{H}}^{\top},

where
\Theta
is the diagonal matrix whose
(j,j)
element is
1-s^{2}_{j}/(s^{2}_{j}+a^{-2}\sigma^{2})
.
Thus, the posterior standard deviation
\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})
can be expressed as

\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})=\sqrt{a^{2}{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{H}}{\bm{\Theta}}{\bm{H}}^{\top}{\bm{x}}}=a\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{H}}^{\top}{\bm{x}}\|.

Hence, using the triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})|

\displaystyle=a|\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{H}}^{\top}{\bm{x}}\|-\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{H}}^{\top}{\bm{y}}\||

\displaystyle\leq a\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{H}}^{\top}{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{H}}^{\top}{\bm{y}}\|

\displaystyle=a\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{H}}^{\top}({\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}})\|.

(E.34)

Noting that
the diagonal element
\theta_{j}
of

{\bm{\Theta}}
satisfies
0\leq\theta_{j}\leq 1
, from
\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|\leq\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{1}
we get

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{H}}^{\top}({\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}})\|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{({\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}})^{\top}{\bm{H}}{\bm{\Theta}}{\bm{H}}^{\top}({\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{({\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}})^{\top}{\bm{H}}{\bm{I}}_{d}{\bm{H}}^{\top}({\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}})}=\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|\leq\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{1}.

(E.35)

Therefore, by substituting (
E.35
) into (
E.34
), we have the desired result.

Next, we show the case of the Gaussian kernel.
From Bochner’s theorem, the Gaussian kernel can be rewritten as follows (see, e.g., section 4.2.1 in
Rasmussen and Williams, 2005
):

\displaystyle k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})=a^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{2\pi{\rm i}({\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}})^{\top}{\bm{\lambda}}}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|^{2}}\text{d}{\bm{\lambda}},

where
{\rm i}
is the imaginary unit.
Furthermore, for each natural number
s\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\mathcal{I}_{s}
and
\mathcal{C}_{s}

be
families of sets given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{s}

\displaystyle=\left\{\left[-s+\frac{j-1}{2^{s}},-s+\frac{j}{2^{s}}\right)\mid j=1,\ldots,2s2^{s}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{s}

\displaystyle=\{I_{1}\times\cdots\times I_{d}\mid I_{1},\ldots,I_{d}\in\mathcal{I}_{s}\}.

In addition, for each element
C_{s,k}=[a^{(1)}_{s,k},b^{(1)}_{s,k})\times\cdots[a^{(d)}_{s,k},b^{(d)}_{s,k})
of

\mathcal{C}_{s}
,
(k=1,\ldots,(2s2^{s})^{d})
, we define the representative point
{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}
of

C_{s,k}
as

{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}=\left(\frac{a^{(1)}_{s,k}+b^{(1)}_{s,k}}{2},\ldots,\frac{a^{(d)}_{s,k}+b^{(d)}_{s,k}}{2}\right)^{\top}=(\lambda_{s,k}^{(1)},\ldots,\lambda_{s,k}^{(d)})^{\top}.

Moreover, let
{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})
be the
(2s2^{s})^{d}
-dimensional vector whose
k
th element
\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}})
is given by

\displaystyle\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}})=ae^{2\pi{\rm i}{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/4}e^{-\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d/2}.

(E.36)

Then, the inner product
\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}),{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{y}})\rangle\equiv\overline{{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})}^{\top}{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{y}})

satisfies

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}),{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{y}})\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{s\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{(2s2^{s})^{d}}a^{2}e^{2\pi{\rm i}(-{\bm{x}}+{\bm{y}})^{\top}{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}

\displaystyle=a^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{2\pi{\rm i}(-{\bm{x}}+{\bm{y}})^{\top}{\bm{\lambda}}}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|^{2}}\text{d}{\bm{\lambda}}

\displaystyle=a^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{2\pi{\rm i}({\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}})^{\top}{\bm{\lambda}}}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|^{2}}\text{d}{\bm{\lambda}}=k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}).

Furthermore, we define
\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})
and
\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})
as

\displaystyle\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}),{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})\rangle-(\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}),{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}_{1})\rangle,\ldots,\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}),{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}_{t})\rangle)

(E.37)

\displaystyle({\bm{K}}_{t,s}+\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{t})^{-1}(\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}),{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}_{1})\rangle,\ldots,\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}),{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}_{t})\rangle)^{\top},

\displaystyle\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=k({\bm{x}},{\bm{x}})-(k({\bm{x}},{\bm{x}}_{1}),\ldots,k({\bm{x}},{\bm{x}}_{t}))({\bm{K}}_{t}+\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{t})^{-1}(k({\bm{x}},{\bm{x}}_{1}),\ldots,k({\bm{x}},{\bm{x}}_{t}))^{\top}

(E.38)

\displaystyle\quad-\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})

(E.39)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}_{t}({\bm{x}})-\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}}),

where
{\bm{K}}_{t,s}
and
{\bm{K}}_{t}
are
t\times t
matrices whose
(i,j)
elements are given by
\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}_{i}),{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}_{j})\rangle
and
\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}_{i}),{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}_{j})\rangle
, respectively.
Then, noting that
\lim_{s\to\infty}\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}),{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{y}})\rangle=k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})
we get

\lim_{s\to\infty}\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})=\sigma^{2}_{t}({\bm{x}}),\quad\lim_{s\to\infty}\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})=0.

We now consider
\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})
and
\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})
.
Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})\geq\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})
.
Then, we have

|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})|=\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}}).

(E.40)

In addition, the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})=\sqrt{\sigma^{2}_{t}({\bm{x}})}=\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})+\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})+|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})|}

(E.41)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})|}.

(E.42)

Similarly, if
\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})>0
, then

\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})
satisfies

\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})=\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{y}})+\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})}\geq\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{y}})}\geq\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{y}})}-\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|}.

On the other hand, if
\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})\leq 0
, then

\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})
satisfies

\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})=\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{y}})+\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})}=\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{y}})-|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|}\geq\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{y}})}-\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|},

where the last inequality is given by
\sqrt{u}-\sqrt{v}\leq\sqrt{u-v}
,
(u\geq v\geq 0)
.
Hence, for both cases, the following holds:

\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})\geq\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{y}})}-\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|}.

(E.43)

Thus, by substituting (
E.42
) and (
E.43
) into (
E.40
), we obtain

|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})|\leq\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})}-\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{y}})}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})|}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|}.

(E.44)

Furthermore, we define the matrix
{\bm{X}}_{t,s}
as
{\bm{X}}_{t,s}=({\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}_{1}),\ldots,{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}_{t}))^{\ast}
,
where
{\bm{A}}^{\ast}
is the conjugate transpose of
{\bm{A}}
.
Then,
\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})
can be rewritten as follows:

\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})=\overline{{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})}^{\top}({\bm{I}}_{(2s2^{s})^{d}}-{\bm{X}}^{\ast}_{t,s}({\bm{X}}_{t,s}{\bm{X}}_{t,s}^{\ast}+\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{t})^{-1}{\bm{X}}_{t,s}){\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}).

Therefore, by using the singular decomposition of
{\bm{X}}_{t,s}
, we have

\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})=\overline{{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})}^{\top}{\bm{U}}{\bm{\Theta}}{\bm{U}}^{\ast}{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}),

where
{\bm{U}}
and
{\bm{\Theta}}
are unitary and diagonal matrices, respectively.
By using the same argument as in the case of the linear kernel, it can be shown that the
(k,k)
element
\theta_{k}

of
{\bm{\Theta}}
satisfies
0\leq\theta_{k}\leq 1
.
Hence, noting that

\check{\sigma}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})=\sqrt{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{t,s}({\bm{x}})}=\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{U}}^{\ast}{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})\|

and (
E.44
), from the triangle inequality we get

\displaystyle|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{U}}^{\ast}{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})\|-\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{U}}^{\ast}{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{y}})\|+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})|}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{U}}^{\ast}{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})-{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{U}}^{\ast}{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{y}})\|+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})|}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|}

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{1/2}{\bm{U}}^{\ast}({\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{y}}))\|+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})|}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{y}})\|+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})|}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|},

(E.45)

where the last inequality is given by
0\leq\theta_{k}\leq 1
.
Moreover, for each
j
with
1\leq j\leq d-1
, let
{\bm{x}}[j]=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{j},x_{j+1},\ldots,x_{d})^{\top}
,
and let
{\bm{x}}[0]\equiv{\bm{x}}
and
{\bm{x}}[d]\equiv{\bm{y}}
.
Then, the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{y}})\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\{{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j])\}\right\|

(E.46)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j])\|.

(E.47)

Thus, by substituting (
E.47
) into (
E.45
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j])\|+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})|}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|}.

(E.48)

In addition, for any
j
and
k
with

1\leq j\leq d
and
1\leq k\leq(2s2^{s})^{d}
, from the definition of
\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}})
we have

\displaystyle\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j])

\displaystyle=aD(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/4}e^{-\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d/2}(e^{2\pi{\rm i}x_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k}}-e^{2\pi{\rm i}y_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k}}),

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi{\rm i}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{j-1},x_{j+1},\ldots,x_{d})({\lambda}^{(1)}_{s,k},\ldots,{\lambda}^{(j-1)}_{s,k},{\lambda}^{(j+1)}_{s,k},\ldots,{\lambda}^{(d)}_{s,k})^{\top}}.

Hence, it follows that

\displaystyle\overline{(\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j]))}(\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j]))

\displaystyle=|\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j])|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq a^{2}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}|e^{2\pi{\rm i}x_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k}}-e^{2\pi{\rm i}y_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k}}|^{2}.

(E.49)

Thus, noting that
|\cos(u)-\cos(v)|\leq|u-v|
and

|\sin(u)-\sin(v)|\leq|u-v|
for any

u,v\in\mathbb{R}
, we get

\displaystyle|e^{2\pi{\rm i}x_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k}}-e^{2\pi{\rm i}y_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k}}|

(E.50)

\displaystyle=|\cos(2\pi x_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k})+{\rm i}\sin(2\pi x_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k})-\cos(2\pi y_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k})-{\rm i}\sin(2\pi y_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k})|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{|\cos(2\pi x_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k})-\cos(2\pi y_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k})|^{2}+|\sin(2\pi x_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k})-\sin(2\pi y_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k})|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{8\pi^{2}\lambda^{(j)2}_{s,k}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}}.

(E.51)

Therefore, by substituting
(
E.51
) into (
E.49
), we obtain

\displaystyle\overline{(\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j]))}(\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j]))

\displaystyle\leq a^{2}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}8\pi^{2}\lambda^{(j)2}_{s,k}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j])\|^{2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{(2s2^{s})^{d}}a^{2}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}8\pi^{2}\lambda^{(j)2}_{s,k}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}.

(E.52)

Moreover, the following holds when
s\to\infty
:

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{(2s2^{s})^{d}}a^{2}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}8\pi^{2}\lambda^{(j)2}_{s,k}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}

\displaystyle=a^{2}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/2}8\pi^{2}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}{\bm{\lambda}}^{\top}{\bm{\lambda}}}\lambda^{2}_{j}\text{d}{\bm{\lambda}}

\displaystyle=a^{2}8\pi^{2}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{1/2}\lambda_{j}^{2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\lambda^{2}_{j}}\text{d}\lambda_{j}\right)\prod_{i\neq j}^{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{1/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\lambda^{2}_{i}}\text{d}\lambda_{i}\right).

By putting
2\pi\rho\lambda_{i}=u_{i}
for each
i
with
1\leq i\leq d
, we have

\displaystyle a^{2}8\pi^{2}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{1/2}\lambda_{j}^{2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\lambda^{2}_{j}}\text{d}\lambda_{j}\right)\prod_{i\neq j}^{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{1/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\lambda^{2}_{i}}\text{d}\lambda_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=a^{2}2\rho^{-2}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(2\pi)^{-1/2}u^{2}_{j}e^{-u^{2}_{j}/2}\text{d}u_{j}\prod_{i\neq j}^{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(2\pi)^{-1/2}e^{-u^{2}_{i}/2}\text{d}u_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2a^{2}}{\rho^{2}}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}.

(E.53)

Thus, (
E.52
) can be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j])\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2a^{2}}{\rho^{2}}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}

(E.54)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{(2s2^{s})^{d}}a^{2}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}8\pi^{2}\lambda^{(j)2}_{s,k}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}-\frac{2a^{2}}{\rho^{2}}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2a^{2}}{\rho^{2}}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}

(E.55)

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\sum_{k=1}^{(2s2^{s})^{d}}a^{2}(2\pi\rho^{2})^{d/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}\rho^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}8\pi^{2}\lambda^{(j)2}_{s,k}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}-\frac{2a^{2}}{\rho^{2}}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{2a^{2}}{\rho^{2}}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}+\tilde{\epsilon}_{s,j},

where
\tilde{\epsilon}_{s,j}
satisfies that
\lim_{s\to\infty}|\tilde{\epsilon}_{s,j}|=0
from (
E.53
).
Hence, we get

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j])\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2a^{2}}{\rho^{2}}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}+\tilde{\epsilon}_{s,j}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}a}{\rho}|x_{j}-y_{j}|+\sqrt{\tilde{\epsilon}_{s,j}}.

(E.56)

By substituting (
E.56
) into (
E.48
), we obtain

|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}a}{\rho}\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sqrt{\tilde{\epsilon}_{s,j}}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})|}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|}.

Furthermore, because the number
s
is an arbitrary natural number, and

\lim_{s\to\infty}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sqrt{\tilde{\epsilon}_{s,j}}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}})|}+\sqrt{|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{y}})|}\right)=0,

we have

|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}a}{\rho}\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{1}.

Finally, we show the case of the Matérn kernel.
From Bochner’s theorem, the Matérn kernel can be rewritten as follows (see, section 4.2.1 in
Rasmussen and Williams, 2005
):

\displaystyle k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})=a^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{2\pi{\rm i}({\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}})^{\top}{\bm{\lambda}}}\frac{2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\Gamma(\nu+d/2)(2\nu)^{\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)\rho^{2\nu}}\left(\frac{2\nu}{\rho^{2}}+4\pi^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|^{2}\right)^{-(\nu+d/2)}\text{d}{\bm{\lambda}}.

For each
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
k
with
k=1,\ldots,(2s2^{s})^{d}
,
we define

\mathcal{I}_{s}
,
\mathcal{C}_{s}
, the element
C_{s,k}=[a^{(1)}_{s,k},b^{(1)}_{s,k})\times\cdots[a^{(d)}_{s,k},b^{(d)}_{s,k})
of

\mathcal{C}_{s}
, and the representative point
{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}
of

C_{s,k}
as in the case of the Gaussian kernel.
Similarly, let
{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}})
be the
(2s2^{s})^{d}
-dimensional vector whose
k
th element
\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}})
is given by

\displaystyle\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}})

(E.57)

\displaystyle=ae^{2\pi{\rm i}{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}}\left(\frac{2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\Gamma(\nu+d/2)(2\nu)^{\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)\rho^{2\nu}}\left(\frac{2\nu}{\rho^{2}}+4\pi^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}\right)^{-(\nu+d/2)}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d/2}.

(E.58)

Then, by using the same argument as in the case of the Gaussian kernel, we obtain the following inequality similar to (
E.48
):

\displaystyle|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j])\|+|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})|,

(E.59)

where
\lim_{s\to\infty}|\epsilon_{t,s}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})|=0
.
Moreover, for any
j
and
k
with
1\leq j\leq d
and
1\leq k\leq(2s2^{s})^{d}
,
from the definition of
\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}})
we get

\displaystyle\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j])

\displaystyle=aD\left(\frac{2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\Gamma(\nu+d/2)(2\nu)^{\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)\rho^{2\nu}}\left(\frac{2\nu}{\rho^{2}}+4\pi^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}\right)^{-(\nu+d/2)}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d/2}

(E.60)

\displaystyle\quad(e^{2\pi{\rm i}x_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k}}-e^{2\pi{\rm i}y_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k}}),

\displaystyle D=e^{2\pi{\rm i}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{j-1},x_{j+1},\ldots,x_{d})({\lambda}^{(1)}_{s,k},\ldots,{\lambda}^{(j-1)}_{s,k},{\lambda}^{(j+1)}_{s,k},\ldots,{\lambda}^{(d)}_{s,k})^{\top}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\overline{(\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j]))}(\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j]))

\displaystyle=|\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j])|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq a^{2}\frac{2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\Gamma(\nu+d/2)(2\nu)^{\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)\rho^{2\nu}}\left(\frac{2\nu}{\rho^{2}}+4\pi^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}\right)^{-(\nu+d/2)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}|e^{2\pi{\rm i}x_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k}}-e^{2\pi{\rm i}y_{j}{\lambda}^{(j)}_{s,k}}|^{2}.

(E.61)

By substituting
(
E.51
) into (
E.61
), we have

\displaystyle\overline{(\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j]))}(\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-\phi_{s,k}({\bm{x}}[j]))

\displaystyle\leq a^{2}\frac{2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\Gamma(\nu+d/2)(2\nu)^{\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)\rho^{2\nu}}\left(\frac{2\nu}{\rho^{2}}+4\pi^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}\right)^{-(\nu+d/2)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}8\pi^{2}\lambda^{(j)2}_{s,k}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j])\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{(2s2^{s})^{d}}a^{2}\frac{2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\Gamma(\nu+d/2)(2\nu)^{\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)\rho^{2\nu}}\left(\frac{2\nu}{\rho^{2}}+4\pi^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}\right)^{-(\nu+d/2)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}8\pi^{2}\lambda^{(j)2}_{s,k}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}.

Furthermore, the following holds when
s\to\infty
:

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{(2s2^{s})^{d}}a^{2}\frac{2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\Gamma(\nu+d/2)(2\nu)^{\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)\rho^{2\nu}}\left(\frac{2\nu}{\rho^{2}}+4\pi^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}\right)^{-(\nu+d/2)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}8\pi^{2}\lambda^{(j)2}_{s,k}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}

\displaystyle=a^{2}8\pi^{2}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\Gamma(\nu+d/2)(2\nu)^{\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)\rho^{2\nu}}\left(\frac{2\nu}{\rho^{2}}+4\pi^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|^{2}\right)^{-(\nu+d/2)}\lambda_{j}^{2}\text{d}{\bm{\lambda}}.

In addition, by putting

2\nu=\tilde{\nu}
and
{\bm{\Sigma}}=(4\pi^{2}\rho^{2})^{-1}{\bm{I}}_{d}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\Gamma(\nu+d/2)(2\nu)^{\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)\rho^{2\nu}}\left(\frac{2\nu}{\rho^{2}}+4\pi^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|^{2}\right)^{-(\nu+d/2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma((\tilde{\nu}+d)/2)}{\Gamma(\tilde{\nu}/2)\tilde{\nu}^{d/2}\pi^{d/2}|{\bm{\Sigma}}|^{1/2}}\left(1+\frac{1}{\tilde{\nu}}{\bm{\lambda}}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}{\bm{\lambda}}\right)^{-(\tilde{\nu}+d)/2}\equiv f({\bm{\lambda}};\tilde{\nu},{\bm{\Sigma}}).

Note that

f({\bm{\lambda}};\tilde{\nu},{\bm{\Sigma}})
is the probability density function of
{\bm{T}}_{\tilde{\nu}}({\bm{0}},{\bm{\Sigma}})
,
where
{\bm{T}}_{\tilde{\nu}}({\bm{0}},{\bm{\Sigma}})
is the multivariate
t
-distribution with
location parameter
{\bm{0}}
, scale matrix
{\bm{\Sigma}}
and

\tilde{\nu}
degrees of freedom.
It is known that the mean vector and covariance matrix of
{\bm{T}}_{\tilde{\nu}}({\bm{0}},{\bm{\Sigma}})
are respectively given by
{\bm{0}}
and

\frac{\tilde{\nu}}{\tilde{\nu}-2}{\bm{\Sigma}}
when

\tilde{\nu}>2
(see, e.g.,
Kotz and Nadarajah, 2004
).
From the assumption
\nu>1
, noting that
\tilde{\nu}=2\nu>2
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\Gamma(\nu+d/2)(2\nu)^{\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)\rho^{2\nu}}\left(\frac{2\nu}{\rho^{2}}+4\pi^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|^{2}\right)^{-(\nu+d/2)}\lambda_{j}^{2}\text{d}{\bm{\lambda}}

(E.62)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f({\bm{\lambda}};\tilde{\nu},{\bm{\Sigma}})\lambda_{j}^{2}\text{d}{\bm{\lambda}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\nu}}{\tilde{\nu}-2}\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}\rho^{2}}=\frac{{\nu}}{{\nu}-1}\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}\rho^{2}}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{(2s2^{s})^{d}}a^{2}\frac{2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\Gamma(\nu+d/2)(2\nu)^{\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)\rho^{2\nu}}\left(\frac{2\nu}{\rho^{2}}+4\pi^{2}\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{s,k}\|^{2}\right)^{-(\nu+d/2)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{s}}\right)^{d}2\lambda^{(j)2}_{s,k}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2a^{2}}{\rho^{2}}(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}\frac{{\nu}}{{\nu}-1}.

Therefore, by using the same argument as in the case of the Gaussian kernel, we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j-1])-{\bm{\phi}}_{s}({\bm{x}}[j])\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}a}{\rho}\sqrt{\frac{\nu}{\nu-1}}|x_{j}-y_{j}|+|\hat{\epsilon}_{s,j}|,

(E.63)

where
\lim_{s\to\infty}|\hat{\epsilon}_{s,j}|=0
.
Hence, by substituting (
E.63
) into (
E.59
), and taking
s\to\infty
we get

|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{y}})|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}a}{\rho}\sqrt{\frac{\nu}{\nu-1}}\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{1}.

∎

The condition that
\sigma^{2}
in Theorem
E.4
is positive is necessary only for the inverse matrix calculation.
Note that
\sigma^{2}
is a user-specified variance parameter of a formal GP model, and is different from the true noise variance.
That is, Theorem
E.4
holds even when the variance of the true noise is zero, i.e., in the noiseless setting.
Also note that
C
in Theorem
E.4
is a constant independent of
\sigma^{2}
.
The result for the Matérn kernel is for the case of
\nu>1
degrees of freedom, and it is a future work to clarify whether the same result holds for
\nu\leq 1
as well.
On the other hand, unfortunately, it can be shown that (
E.29
) does not hold for
\nu=1/2
, which is often used in practice for Matérn kernels.

Theorem E.5
.

In the setting of Theorem
E.4
, the Matérn kernel with
\nu=1/2
does not satisfy (
E.29
).

Proof.

Let
C
be an arbitrary positive number.
The Matérn kernel with
\nu=1/2
is given by

k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})=a^{2}\exp(-\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|/\rho).

In addition, suppose that
{\bm{x}}_{1}=\cdots={\bm{x}}_{t}={\bm{0}}
.
Moreover, we define
{\bm{K}}_{t}
as

{\bm{K}}_{t}=a^{2}{\bm{1}}_{t}{\bm{1}}^{\top}_{t}+\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{t}.

Then, the inverse matrix
{\bm{K}}^{-1}_{t}
can be expressed as

{\bm{K}}^{-1}_{t}=\sigma^{-2}{\bm{I}}_{t}-\frac{\frac{a^{2}}{\sigma^{4}}{\bm{1}}_{t}{\bm{1}}^{\top}_{t}}{1+\frac{a^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}t}.

Therefore, the posterior variance at point
{\bm{0}}
is given by

\sigma^{2}_{t}({\bm{0}})=a^{2}-a^{4}{\bm{1}}^{\top}_{t}{\bm{K}}^{-1}_{t}{\bm{1}}_{t}=a^{2}-a^{4}\frac{t}{\sigma^{2}+a^{2}t}=\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\sigma^{2}+a^{2}t}.

Next, let
s
be a number with

0<s<\rho/2
, and let
{\bm{x}}=(s,0,\ldots,0)^{\top}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{t}({\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=a^{2}-a^{4}\exp(-2s/\rho){\bm{1}}^{\top}_{t}{\bm{K}}^{-1}_{t}{\bm{1}}_{t}

\displaystyle=a^{2}-a^{4}\exp(-2s/\rho)\frac{t}{\sigma^{2}+a^{2}t}

\displaystyle=a^{2}-a^{4}(1+\exp(-2s/\rho)-1)\frac{t}{\sigma^{2}+a^{2}t}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}_{t}({\bm{0}})+\frac{a^{4}t}{\sigma^{2}+a^{2}t}(1-\exp(-2s/\rho))

(E.64)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}_{t}({\bm{0}})\left\{1+\frac{a^{4}t}{\sigma^{2}_{t}({\bm{0}})(\sigma^{2}+a^{2}t)}(1-\exp(-2s/\rho))\right\}\equiv\sigma^{2}_{t}({\bm{0}})(1+u).

Thus, from

u\geq 0
we get

|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})|=\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})=\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})\sqrt{1+u}-\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}}).

Furthermore, by using Taylor’s expansion of

f(u)=\sqrt{1+u}
at point
u=0
, we obtain

\sqrt{1+u}\geq 1+\frac{1}{2}u-\frac{1}{8}u^{2}.

Moreover, for each
t
, there exists a number
s
such that
0<s<\rho/2
and
u\leq 1
.
Therefore, it follows that

\sqrt{1+u}\geq 1+\frac{1}{2}u-\frac{1}{8}u^{2}\geq 1+\frac{1}{2}u-\frac{1}{8}u=1+\frac{3}{8}u.

By using this, we have

|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})|\geq\frac{3}{8}u\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})=\frac{3}{8}\frac{a^{4}t}{\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})(\sigma^{2}+a^{2}t)}(1-\exp(-2s/\rho)).

In addition, noting that

\exp(-2s/\rho)\leq 1-2s/\rho+(2s/\rho)^{2}/2
and
1-s/\rho\geq 1/2
, we get

1-\exp(-2s/\rho)\geq 2s/\rho-(2s/\rho)^{2}/2=2s/\rho(1-s/\rho)\geq s/\rho.

Therefore, the following inequality holds:

|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})|\geq\frac{3}{8}\frac{a^{4}t/\rho}{\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})(\sigma^{2}+a^{2}t)}s=\frac{3}{8}\frac{a^{4}/\rho}{\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})(\sigma^{2}/t+a^{2})}\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{0}}\|_{1}.

Hence, since
\lim_{t\to\infty}\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})=0
, the following inequality holds for sufficiently large
t
:

|\sigma_{t}({\bm{x}})-\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})|\geq\frac{3}{8}\frac{a^{4}/\rho}{\sigma_{t}({\bm{0}})(\sigma^{2}/t+a^{2})}\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{0}}\|_{1}>C\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{0}}\|_{1}.

∎

F
Details of the Experimental Settings and Pseudo-codes

In this section, we describe the experimental settings.

F.1
Common Settings

We used a multi-start L-BFGS-B method
Byrd et al., 1995
(SciPy
Virtanen et al., 2020
implementation) to perform various optimization such as optimizing AFs, finding the optimal value of synthetic functions.
First, we sample
1000
initial points using Latin hypercube sampling (LHS)
McKay et al., 2000
.
Then, we run L-BFGS-B with parameter
\mathtt{ftol}=10^{-3},\mathtt{gtol}=10^{-3}
for each initial point and pick the top 5 results.
Finally, we run L-BFGS-B with default parameters for these five results and return the best result.
We implemented GP models and all the comparison methods mainly using PyTorch
Paszke et al., 2019
and GPyTorch
Gardner et al., 2018
.
By utilizing the automatic differentiation of PyTorch, we can easily apply gradient methods to optimize AFs.

F.2
Comparison Methods

CBO

In CBO, a scalar output is assumed for each stage.
For each iteration
t
, it first chooses the controllable parameter of the final stage
\bm{x}^{(N)}_{t}
and desired output of previous stage
y^{(N-1)}_{\mathrm{desire}}
by maximizing EI:

(y^{(N-1)}_{\mathrm{desire}},\bm{x}^{(N)}_{t})=\argmax_{y^{(N-1)},\bm{x}^{(N)}}\sigma^{(N)}_{t-1}(y^{(N-1)},\bm{x}^{(N)})(Z\Phi(Z)+\phi(Z)),

(F.1)

where
\Phi,\phi
are the cumulative distribution function and probability density function of the standard normal distribution, respectively,

Z=0
if
\sigma^{(N)}_{t-1}(y^{(N-1)},\bm{x}^{(N)})=0
and be
Z=(\mu_{t-1}^{(N)}(y^{(N-1)},\bm{x}^{(N)})-F_{\mathrm{best}})/\sigma^{(N)}_{t-1}(y^{(N-1)},\bm{x}^{(N)})
otherwise.
We could not find any description about the range of optimization parameters in
Dai Nguyen et al., 2016
.
We used
\mathcal{X}^{(N)}
for the range of
\bm{x}^{(N)}
, and we used the range twice as wide as the actual range for the range of
y^{(N-1)}
, which is supposed to be unknown.
Then, CBO chooses
(y^{(N-2)}_{\mathrm{desire}},\bm{x}_{t}^{(N-1)})
of stage
N-1
as follows:

(y^{(N-2)}_{\mathrm{desire}},\bm{x}^{(N-1)}_{t})=\argmin_{y^{(N-2)},\bm{x}^{(N-1)}}\left(\kappa_{1}v^{-1}+\kappa_{2}v\right)\|m-y_{\mathrm{desire}}^{(N-1)}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathrm{cost}(y^{(N-2)},\bm{x}^{(N-1)}),

(F.2)

where
m=\mu_{t-1}^{(N-1)}(y^{(N-2)},\bm{x}^{(N-1)}),\;v=\sigma^{(N-1)\,2}_{t-1}(y^{(N-2)},\bm{x}^{(N-1)})
,
\mathrm{cost}(\cdot)
is the cost function, and
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}
are hyperparameters.
By repeating this operation, a controllable parameter of stage 1
\bm{x}_{t}^{(1)}
is determined finally.
We used
\mathrm{cost}(\cdot)=0
for simplicity and set
\kappa_{1}=1,\kappa_{2}=1
.

In the solar cell simulator experiments, output of stage
1
and stage
2
are vectors.
To deal with vector output, we replace a predictive mean and variance in
eq.

F.2
with a mean vector and covariance matrix.
Therefore, for the vector output setting, the following AF was used instead of
eq.

F.2
:

(\bm{y}^{(n-1)}_{\mathrm{desire}},\bm{x}^{(n)}_{t})=\argmin_{\bm{y}^{(n-1)},\bm{x}^{(x)}}\Bigl[\left(\bm{m}-\bm{y}_{\mathrm{desire}}^{(n)}\right)^{\top}\left(\kappa_{1}{\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}}+\kappa_{2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\left(\bm{m}-\bm{y}_{\mathrm{desire}}^{(n)}\right)\Bigr],

(F.3)

where
\bm{m}=\bm{\mu}_{t-1}^{(n)}(\bm{y}^{(n-1)},\bm{x}^{(n)})
and
\bm{\Sigma}
is a diagonal matrix whose
(i,i)
-th element is defined as
\sigma^{(n)\,2}_{m,t-1}(\bm{y}^{(n-1)},\bm{x}^{(n)})
.

FB-EI, FB-UCB

In FB-EI and FB-UCB, the next sampling point is determined by using a fully black-box GP model.
To construct this model, we employed an ARD Gaussian kernel and set the noise variance of GP to
10^{-4}
.
The kernel parameters were estimated by maximizing the marginal likelihood.
In particular, FB-UCB used GP-UCB method
Srinivas et al., 2010
, and we set its exploration parameter
\beta_{\mathrm{GP-UCB}}^{1/2}=2
.

EI-based

Since EI-based AF is computed through sampling, we have to use stochastic gradient methods to optimize it in a naive implementation.
However, L-BFGS-B can also be applied by utilizing reparameterization-trick
Kingma and Welling, 2014
.
At the beginning of the optimization, we draw
base-samples

\bm{\omega}^{(n)}\in\mathbb{R}^{S}
from standard multivariate Gaussian distribution for each middle stage.
Then, instead of sampling each
\{y_{s}^{(n)}\}_{s=1}^{S}
directly from Gaussian distribution, we sample it as follows:

y^{(n)}_{s}=\mu_{y^{(n)}_{s}}+\sigma_{y^{(n)}_{s}}\omega^{(n)}_{s}.

(F.4)

Here,
\mu_{y^{(n)}_{s}},\sigma_{y^{(n)}_{s}}
are the mean and standard deviation of the Gaussian distribution that follows
y^{(n)}_{s}
, respectively.
The EI-based AF becomes a deterministic and differentiable function with the above modifications, and the L-BFGS-B method can be applied.
Moreover, EI-based AF can also be applied to the vector output setting.
We only need to change it to sample
\bm{y}^{(n)}_{s}
instead of
y^{(n)}_{s}
in the middle stage.

In EI-SUS-R for the suspension setting experiments, we applied the stock reduction rule (13) except for the stock obtained in the last iteration.

F.3
Synthetic functions and Solar Cell Simulator

Sample Paths:

In the sample path experiments, we used random Fourier features (RFFs) to draw continuous functions from GP priors.
We first sampled 1000 RFFs and built Bayesian linear regression (BLR) model.
From the BLR model, we sampled weight parameters and constructed functions.

Rosenbrock Function:

For any
d\geq 2
,
d
-dimensional Rosenbrock function is defined as follows:

f(\bm{x})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(100(x_{i+1}-x_{i}^{2})^{2}+(x_{i}-1)^{2}\right).

(F.5)

In our experiments, we used negative Rosenbrock functions, which are multiplied by
-1
.

Sphere Function:

For any
d\geq 2
,
d
-dimensional Sphere function is defined as follows:

f(\bm{x})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}x_{i}^{2}.

(F.6)

In our experiments, we used negative Sphere functions, which are multiplied by
-1
.

Matyas Function:

Matyas function (
d=2
) is defined as follows:

f(\bm{x})=0.26(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})-0.48x_{1}x_{2}.

(F.7)

In our experiments, we used negative Matyas functions, which are multiplied by
-1
.

Solar Cell Simulator:

The simulators for stages one and two are Python implementations of the physical models described in Section 4 of
Bentzen, 2006
.
The simulator of stage three is based on PC1Dmod 6.2
Haug and Greulich, 2016
, which is the software for simulating solar cells.
We confirmed that PC1D sometimes caused errors due to convergence failure of the internal calculations.
However, standard BO frameworks cannot handle the situation where the observation fails.
Thus, we used a kernel ridge regression model constructed using the data collected from PC1D as the simulator of stage 3.
To create this simulator, we ran PC1D on each of the 2000 input points sampled using the LHS, and used the 1935 data points among them that could be run without error.

Hydrogen plasma treatment process:

The real-world datasets for the first and second stages are from
(
Miyagawa et al., 2021a
)
and the simulator in
https://www.pvlighthouse.com.au/equivalent-circuit
, respectively.
For both stages, we fitted the GPs with a Gaussian kernel, in which hyperparameters are selected by the marginal likelihood maximization.
Then, as with sample paths, we sampled 1000 RFFs, built BLR models, and generate continuous sample paths once.
We used these sample paths as the surrogate objectives.

F.4
Pseudo-codes of the proposed methods

We describe the proposed method of
section

3
in
algorithm

1
.
Additionally, we also describe the proposed method of extension setting in
algorithm

2
.

Input:

Initial data
\{\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(n)}\}_{n=1}^{N}
,
\beta
,
\eta_{t}

for

t=0,N,2N,\dots,T

do

1

Fit GP models using
\{\mathcal{D}_{t-1}^{(n)}\}_{n=1}^{N}

for

n=1,\ldots,N

do

2

Select
\bm{x}^{(n)}_{t+n}
by maximizing
eq.

6c
or
eq.

15

Observe output
\bm{y}_{t+n}^{(n)}
corresponding input
(\bm{y}^{(n-1)}_{t+n-1},\bm{x}^{(n)}_{t+n})

\mathcal{D}_{t+n}^{(n)}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}_{t+n-1}^{(n)}\cup\{((\bm{y}^{(n-1)}_{t+n-1},\bm{x}_{t+n}^{(n)}),\bm{y}_{t+n}^{(n)})\}

end for

3

t\leftarrow t+N

end for

4

Output:

Estimated solution
\hat{\bm{x}}_{t}^{(1)},\dots,\hat{\bm{x}}_{t}^{(N)}

Algorithm 1

Cascade process optimization in sequential observation

Input:

\{\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(n)}\}_{n=1}^{N}
,
\beta
,
\eta_{t}
, stage cost
\{\lambda^{(n)}\}_{n=1}^{N}
, budget
\lambda_{\max}

t\leftarrow 1
,
\mathcal{S}_{t}^{(0)}\leftarrow\{\bm{0}\},\{\mathcal{S}_{t}^{(n)}\leftarrow\emptyset\}_{n=1}^{N-1}
, spend cost
\lambda\leftarrow 0

while

\lambda\leq\lambda_{\max}

do

1

Fit GP models using
\{\mathcal{D}_{t-1}^{(n)}\}_{n=1}^{N}

Select
n_{t},\bm{y}_{t}^{(n_{t}-1)},\bm{x}_{t}^{(n)}
by
eq.

21

Observe
\bm{y}_{t}^{(n_{t})}

\mathcal{D}_{t}^{(n_{t})}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}_{t-1}^{(n_{t})}\cup\{((\bm{y}_{t}^{(n_{t}-1)},\bm{x}_{t}^{(n_{t})}),\bm{y}_{t}^{(n_{t})})\}

Remove stock
\mathcal{S}_{t}^{(n_{t}-1)}\leftarrow\mathcal{S}_{t}^{(n_{t}-1)}\setminus\bm{y}_{t}^{(n_{t}-1)}

if

n_{t}\neq N

then

2

Add observed stock
\mathcal{S}_{t}^{(n_{t})}\leftarrow\mathcal{S}_{t}^{(n_{t})}\cup\{\bm{y}_{t}^{(n_{t})}\}

\lambda\leftarrow\lambda+\lambda^{(n_{t})},\;t\leftarrow t+1

end while

3

Output:

Estimated solution
\hat{\bm{x}}_{t}^{(1)},\dots,\hat{\bm{x}}_{t}^{(N)}

Algorithm 2

Cascade process optimization in suspension setting

:
Results of comparison between
EI-based
and EI-FN

.

G
Additional Experimental Results

We additionally show the comparison between
EI-based
AF and EI-FN
Astudillo and Frazier, 2021
.
In this experiment, we used a three- and five-stage cascade consisting of GP pre-distributed sample paths.
We set
\ell_{d}^{(n)}=1,\ell_{w}^{(n)}=1
, and the other experimental settings are the same as those described in
Section

6
.

Figure

7
shows the results of 20 runs with different random seeds.
Since the parameters
\ell_{d}^{(n)}
and
\ell_{w}^{(n)}
are relatively small, the sample paths can be sensitive to the input uncertainty.
Therefore, in this experiment,
EI-based
AF that performs adaptive decision-making using intermediate observations clearly outperforms EI-FN, which is nonadaptive.
